Entries below record evidence at the time of each pass. Follow [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md) and the [agent documentation contract](../AGENT-DOCUMENTATION.md) when updating this file.

## Battlefield orders through MCP

The battlefield census exposes player runtime keys. The single-unit state query reports position, command, target and optional goal proximity. Use it with existing native orders and bounded sequence waits via the [battlefield automation recipe](../../.agents/skills/rogue-command-bepinex-mcp/references/automation.md#battlefield-orders). The connected server advertised the tool, local sequence coverage confirmed one dispatch followed by observations, and the Release loader was deployed with a verified hash. Steam behavior remains unverified.

- Isolated each canonical Level 2 module build/LOD/export inside a per-module `work_scene` in `tools/Build-CanonicalVisualModules.py`, replaced `bpy.ops` primitives/bevels/mode switches with pure-BMesh helpers (`_create_bmesh_object` with explicit `matrix_world`, `_apply_bmesh_angle_bevel`, `temp_override` + active-mesh guards for `shade_smooth_by_angle` and `modifier_apply`) in `tools/RogueModelKit.py` (byte-identical skill copy preserved), added `assume_cache_optimized` to `tools/functional_engineering_integration.py` to skip redundant post-LOD Forsyth solves, and vectorized `tools/mesh_geometry_algorithms.py` (`foreach_get`/`foreach_set`, `np.unique`, lazy `heapq` + LUT `compute_forsyth_face_order`). ## Coordinated field support

Squad support scores healable health and repairable armor deficits. Support orders approach their assigned ally and use native Heal or RepairArmor within four world units. Release and 70 tactical checks passed locally. Steam execution remains unverified.

Support movement refresh replaces the current tagged approach while preserving pending native commands. Arrival stops that approach, releases its lease and applies treatment. Direct treatment consumes a dispatch slot and leaves existing unrelated order ownership intact.

## Overlapping coordinated AI profiles

Dispatch preserves an active coordinated order whose native tag matches a lease held by another active profile. Once the order finishes or changes, the guard no longer blocks dispatch. Release and 70 tactical checks passed locally. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated idle-only orders

Dispatch reads the live native command queue before issuing an idle-only order. Unavailable queue reads suppress dispatch. Release and 70 tactical checks pass locally, including an order queued after snapshot collection. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated command completion

The composite checks live command ownership before arbitration. A completed or replaced order releases its lease so the unit can act again. Release and 70 tactical checks passed locally, including a retreat that finishes during its lockout. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated command alternatives

The arbiter retains same-unit alternatives after a rejected dispatch or lease check. Eligible alternatives compete globally by utility and still observe one successful order per unit and the dispatch budget. Release builds cleanly and 70 tactical checks pass locally. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated command lease timing

Runtime arbitration grants a lease and counts an approved order after successful dispatch. A rejected winner leaves the existing lease intact and the slot available to another unit. Release and 70 tactical checks passed locally. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated skill dispatch conditions

Dispatch rechecks live health and horizontal range. Self skills triggered by nearby enemies retain the motivating hostile ID for the live sight check. Release builds cleanly and the tactical suite passes 70/70. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated self-skill activation

EnemiesInRange self skills require a hostile within native sight. The coordinated tactical suite passes 70/70, including the self-cast scoring gate. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated native skill range

The battlefield collector uses native skill range and unrestricted location casting rules. Both coordinated directors consume that range. The tactical suite passes 70/70, including a target beyond skill range but inside weapon range. Steam execution remains unverified.

## Coordinated tactical skill dispatch

- Dispatch uses live SkillInfo to select self, entity, or location commands and rechecks mana, queued activation, and hostile target availability. Release builds cleanly and the tactical AI suite passes 70/70. Steam execution remains unverified.

## General capability audit — native gathering search radius

- Positive crystal resourceGathering.gatherRange now filters the native Harvest candidate list by Euclidean grid-cell radius around `_givenHarvestPosition ?? _self.Position`. Boundary cells remain; zero preserves native unrestricted search. Native claim exclusion, nearest/path selection, movement, refinery delivery and Stop on empty candidates remain intact. The global grid crystal list is never changed.
- Unit profile registration carries ResourceGathering even when range is its only mechanic. Central patch plan installs NativeHarvestRangePatch for the native Harvest type; typed field/signature drift or anything other than one List<Vector2Int>.AddRange site aborts installation. The callback caches field access and contains exceptions.
- Rechecked pinned game identity. Offline native IL decoder/probe accepted the actual 343-byte Harvest method: 112 instructions to 114, one filter call; log `artifacts/general-capability-audit/gather-range-native-il.log`. The standalone .NET 8 Harmony instruction reader reported Body-less method, so this evidence uses raw MethodBody decoding and is not a live Harmony installation claim.
- Release/harness builds: zero warnings/errors. Full C# suite: 590 passed, zero failed, one Windows file-symlink-privilege skip. Full Python all-mode: 768 tests, successful true. Tests cover native callback/profile/destination resolution, radius boundary, preserved order, zero/empty range results and drift rejection. Logs `artifacts/general-capability-audit/gather-range-*`. Steam execution remains unverified.
- Deployment backup/hash manifest: `artifacts/general-capability-audit/gather-range-deployment/manifest.json`.

## 2026-11-14 Material Maker GodotSteam load and API errors

- **Root cause:** The packed Material Maker project includes `addons/godotsteam/godotsteam.gdextension`, whose `windows.debug.x86_64` mapping requires the debug DLL. The CLI runtime had only the release DLL beside the PCK, so Godot's Windows loader could not open the selected native library. Once the extension loaded, `material_maker/steam.gd` called `initAuthentication()` and `isSubscribed()` even when `steamInitEx()` returned status 1 (`ConnectToGlobalUser failed` in this non-Steam CLI run).
- **Repair:** `tools/Build-MaterialMakerCli.ps1` now stages the manifest, both official Windows x64 debug/release GodotSteam DLLs, and `steam_api64.dll` beside the PCK, verifies each SHA-256, and records the files in `build-manifest.json`. `tools/patch_material_maker_cli_shutdown.py` keeps the extension, validates its mappings and native files, and makes `steam.gd` stop dependent calls when Steam initialization returns a nonzero status. This supersedes the 2026-11-13 entry's proposal to drop GodotSteam from the isolated runtime; keep the extension and stage its required files.
- **GUT lifecycle correction:** The headless import had one genuine warning, `Canceling suspended execution of "_enter_tree" due to a script reload`. Pinned GUT 9.5.0 awaited a one-second timer inside `EditorPlugin._enter_tree()`, then Godot reloaded the script before that coroutine resumed. The patcher now preserves GUT in `project.godot`, keeps the timer in GUI mode, and skips only that GUI startup wait when `DisplayServer.get_name()` is `headless`. This fixes the cancellation at its source; no warning was filtered or GUT plugin disabled.
- **Build:** `tools/Build-MaterialMakerCli.ps1` passed for pinned Material Maker 1.7 / Godot 4.7 after the GUT correction. PCK SHA-256: `7C1EAE3C4D5769EF8D408A8D68A23DC56DAC4D9302A37A7CABA1934FD2484820`. Import and pack stderr are empty; complete stdout/stderr have no `ERROR:`, `WARNING:`, GDExtension failure, shader/script error, or resource/thread leak. `project.godot` still enables `res://addons/gut/plugin.cfg`. Build manifest and complete logs: `artifacts/mm-warning-fix/build-20261114T072030Z/`.
- **Medal registry repair:** The duplicate hook warning was caused by putting both architecture-specific manifests in the shared current-user layer key. A reversible backup was written to `artifacts/mm-warning-fix/registry-architecture-test-20261114T002826/`. The x64 manifest now lives at `HKCU\Software\Khronos\Vulkan\ImplicitLayers`; the x86 manifest lives at `HKCU\Software\WOW6432Node\Khronos\Vulkan\ImplicitLayers`. `vulkaninfo --summary` from both `System32` and `SysWOW64` exits 0, lists the Medal layer once, and has no duplicate warning. No system-wide registration or manifest was changed.
- **Export:** After the registry repair, the strict procedural bake of `content/source-art/bulwarkfoundry-material.ptex` passed `tactical-hd-v2` at 2048×2048 with zero quality failures. All four PNG SHA-256 values match `artifacts/mm-warning-fix/gut-fixed-strict-export/` and the earlier verified export; the `.ptex` and production PNGs were not changed. Full logs: `artifacts/mm-warning-fix/host-registry-repaired-export/`.
- **Remaining host warning:** The latest `material-maker-error.log` contains only `windows_read_data_files_in_registry: Registry lookup failed to get layer manifest files.` No explicit-layer manifest is registered on this machine. Khronos loader source shows that an empty registry lookup is logged as a warning but returned as success; it is optional layer discovery, not a failed driver, graph compile or export. Do not add a dummy layer or disable registry discovery just to suppress it. `vulkaninfo` also reports OBS/RTSS/Medal layer API-version compatibility warnings when requesting Vulkan 1.4; Material Maker's current log does not report these. Keep full logs. See [Windows registry lookup](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/Vulkan-Loader/blob/main/loader/loader_windows.c#L3453-L3500) and [duplicate-layer handling](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/Vulkan-Loader/blob/main/loader/loader.c#L3449-L3467).
- **Boundary:** Only the isolated `.tools/MaterialMaker-1.7-patched/` runtime and ignored `artifacts/` outputs changed; `.ptex` and production PNGs remain unchanged. The game was not started. No Blender visual review, Unity import, or in-game appearance claim is made.

## General capability audit — command follower ownership and range

- Command pulses no longer count or buff a follower owned by another captain. Each director tracks its links and releases followers that leave range or become ineligible; returning followers can rejoin. Profile refresh and runtime cleanup release owned links without taking another captain's followers.
- Regression extends the existing reuse scenario with a one-follower cap, a foreign-owned candidate, departure and re-entry. Release and harness builds: zero warnings/errors. ## General capability audit — retreat recovery lifecycle

- Retreat-and-regroup tracks its dispatched combatants and releases tagged current orders when average group health recovers, eligibility is lost, or the module shuts down. Pending native orders and foreign current orders are preserved.
- Recovery checks retain the two-second scan independently of dispatch cooldown; cooldown still prevents rapid repeat retreats. Existing average-health/base scope and attack-move regroup behavior are retained.

- Deployment backup and hash manifest: `artifacts/general-capability-audit/retreat-deployment/manifest.json`.

## General capability audit — persistent harvester escorts

- Harvester escort keeps one capped squad across scans instead of recruiting another `maxEscorts` defenders while previous escorts remain active. Existing escorts are preferred; lost/ineligible units can be replaced.
- Destination drift refreshes the owned current order without stacking old commands. Threat disappearance and shutdown release owned orders while preserving queued native orders and unrelated current commands. Threat selection checks native hostility.
- Release build: zero warnings/errors. Focused AiTacticalModules suite: 68/68 passed, covering repeated scans, moving threats, threat departure and foreign-command ownership. Local evidence only; Steam/profile execution remains unverified.
- Deployment backup and hash manifest: `artifacts/general-capability-audit/escort-deployment/manifest.json`.

## General capability audit — scoped factory reinforcements

- Factory reinforcement now counts only live units in its selected AI base, and reserves army slots for all in-scope factory queues before adding production. Base `-1` retains the shared AI scope. Losses release capacity on the next scan.
- Production dispatch counts actual queue growth. Free reinforcement waits for an existing queue to empty because native free Produce replaces/refunds an existing entry.
- Release build: zero warnings/errors. Focused AiTacticalModules suite: 67/67 passed, including scoped pending-capacity, subsequent scans, losses and free-production continuation. Local evidence only; Steam and active stage/profile execution remain unverified.
- Deployment backup/hash manifest: `artifacts/general-capability-audit/reinforcement-deployment/manifest.json`.

## General capability audit — repairer pursuit lifecycle

- Repairers refresh their owned movement destination on repair ticks after at least one meter of target drift. Replacements cancel only the current owned move, preserving pending native orders.
- Arrival, no eligible target, and module shutdown release owned movement. Unrelated current commands remain untouched.
- Release build: zero warnings/errors. Focused AiTacticalModules harness: 66/66 passed, including moving-target, recovery and shutdown behavior. This is local evidence; active stage binding and Steam behavior remain unverified.

## 2026-11-14 Python test suite restructuring & optimization

- **Layout:** Moved 69 loose root `tests/python/test_*.py` modules into domain subpackages (`mcp/`, `packs/`, `pbr/`, `modeling/`, `runtime/`, plus additions to `balance/`, `bundles/`, `registry/`, `schema/`), leaving `helpers.py`, `__init__.py`, and `test_run_python_tests.py` at root. All moved depth-sensitive files now resolve `REPO_ROOT` / `TOOLS_DIR` / `CONTENT_DIR` through `tests.python.helpers`.
- **Optimizations:**
  - `tools/validate_native_visual_roots.py` (`NativeHierarchy`): replaced eager `parse_as_dict()` over all 106,910 GameObjects with fast `peek_name()` indexing and lazy component/transform lookup.
  - `tests/python/helpers.py`: added `BLENDER_EXE`, `GAME_ROOT`, `read_text_cached`, and `parse_ast_cached` (keeping `read_json` strictly uncached) and shared AST/text parses across `RogueModelKit.py` contract specs.
  - `tests/python/schema/test_structured_unit_parameter_schema.py` & `tests/python/bundles/test_mech_rigging_bundle.py`: validate minimal 1-unit fixtures for field-mutation loops and reuse loaded `UnityPy` environments per bundle.
  - `tests/python/authoring/test_gravitas_hardflow_recipe.py`: load `gravitas-breaker.blend` once in `setUpClass` instead of per-test `setUp`.
  - `tools/bepinex_mcp_reload.py`: always restore `sys.modules["build_bepinex_mcp"]` in `finally:` so fake reload tests never leak across sequentially executed worker modules.
  - `tests/python/mcp/test_bepinex_mcp_shared.py`: added `_wait_for_pid_exit` using Windows `OpenProcess` synchronization + bounded `worker.log` cleanup wait so venv launcher stubs cannot cause `WinError 32` temp-dir failures.
  - `tools/run_python_tests.py` & `tools/run_python_checks.{ps1,sh}`: added `moduleSeconds` per-module timing, LPT weighted shard balancing in `partition_unit()`, single-process sequential Blender (`bpy`) batching with `read_factory_settings(use_empty=True)` between modules (parallel with unit shards for `--jobs >= 2`, serial for `--jobs 1`), and `--factory-startup` in Blender bake/budget subprocess tests.
- **Verification:** `.venv` `python -m tools.run_python_tests --output artifacts/python-tests/report.json` passed **749/749** tests (`748 passed, 1 skipped`, `successful: true`, wall time `25.43s`; system Python `24.17s` vs `36.05s` baseline). `git diff --check -- ':!unity/'` clean.

## 2026-11-14 Material Maker placeholder shader repair

- **Root cause:** The verbose Godot pack trace places all six `get_shader_parameter_list` errors immediately after loading `material_maker/windows/export_animation/export_animation.gd` and while serializing `export_animation.tscn`. That scene had three `Shader` subresources (IDs `1`, `3`, and `10`) with empty `code`, assigned to three `ShaderMaterial` placeholders. Godot's headless renderer could not enumerate parameters for those incomplete shader resources.
- **Repair:** `tools/patch_material_maker_cli_shutdown.py` now initializes each placeholder with valid transparent `canvas_item` shader code. `ExportAnimation.set_source()` later assigns the generated animation preview shader to those same resources. This fixes the incomplete scene resources; no log filtering, diagnostic caps, or error suppression was added.
- **Build evidence:** `tools/Setup-TextureAuthoringTools.ps1 -MaterialMaker` passed. Latest manifest and logs: `artifacts/mm-warning-fix/build-20261114T045233Z/`. The PCK SHA-256 is `2A459B6DFC5E52EA8C6C3295BCAF6A6DB4DFF7D4CE831E262EAF981408C93D69`; `godot-pack.stderr.log` is empty and the latest import/pack logs contain no warning/error markers. Earlier six errors came from `export_animation.tscn`; the final pack still includes the scene and all its subresources.
- **Export evidence:** Strict procedural bake with `content/source-art/bulwarkfoundry-material.ptex` passed `qualityGate.passed=true` at 2048×2048 in `artifacts/mm-warning-fix/final-20261114T0453Z/`. All four output PNGs match the prior `final-20261114T0434Z` bake byte-for-byte. The latest `material-maker-error.log` is empty; the host Vulkan warnings from earlier runs did not recur. The source PTeX and production maps were not changed.
- **Boundary:** This is local setup/pack and CLI export evidence. The GUI animation-export window was not opened, so its visual preview is statically verified through the `set_source()` assignment path rather than interactively exercised. The game was not started.
- **Skill synchronization:** Updated `.agents/skills/rogue-command-material-maker-texture-authoring/SKILL.md` with the root cause and correction. Preserve all diagnostics; if new warnings appear, trace and repair their source rather than hiding them.

## 2026-11-13 MCP endpoint concurrency bounds

- The HTTP frontend bounds concurrent connections at 32, request-body inactivity at 10 seconds, and gate waits at 120 seconds. Gate timeout reports that the request was not started. A dropped client during response delivery cannot trigger a second response or a backend replay. The active HTTP process must restart to adopt frontend source changes; no in-game validation was performed.
- Local focused transport tests pass. The full suite report `artifacts/mcp-concurrency/full.json` recorded 732 passes and one skip against the then-current tree, including concurrent unrelated test changes. Antigravity skill was synchronized.

## 2026-11-13 Shared MCP connection resilience

- The shared worker rejects malformed envelopes and survives failed named-pipe authentication or invalid JSON without exiting. The HTTP endpoint rejects non-ASCII invalid credentials without a `compare_digest` exception. Local process tests verify the same worker PID responds after these failures; no game run was started. Worker and frontend process source changes require their respective processes to restart to take effect.

## 2026-11-13 Reloadable MCP reader

- Server reader code and transport limits now reload before each read, alongside handlers and schema definitions. A blocked read completes on its previous reader; the next read adopts the new implementation. Catalog changes discovered during reader refresh remain pending until dispatch emits the notification. Framing errors are recognized across reloaded class identities and close damaged streams.
- Local tests edit reader behavior in a real stdio process and verify adoption through the same pipes and PID. Existing processes with the old fixed loop need one bootstrap reload to install this mechanism; subsequent server-reader edits do not. Reload host/launcher and game DLL changes remain outside this mechanism. No game was started.

## 2026-11-13 MCP request boundary hardening

- Invalid RPC envelopes and non-object params/arguments are rejected before dispatch. Tool notifications cannot execute operations without a result channel. Zero-valued IDs remain valid. Reload dispatch handles invalid non-object input without crashing its response path.
- Stdio messages are limited to 16 MiB, framed headers to 8 KiB. Invalid framing returns a parse error and closes the transport rather than interpreting trailing bytes as another command. Parser/host edits require one client process reload. Local tests cover these cases; no game was launched.

## 2026-11-13 Persistent MCP handler reload

- Stdio keeps a persistent process and reloads server/schema handlers between requests on source-content changes. Code is compiled from the fingerprinted bytes without stale pycache; failed reloads reject the current request and can recover after source repair. Diagnostics instance IDs reset per request, not during an in-flight sequence. Schema changes notify the client through tools/list_changed; client schema refresh remains client-dependent.
- 71 local tests pass, including a real stdio process that observes an edited server version through the same pipes/PID, cache invalidation, invalid-source rejection and repair. No game was started. Existing pre-host processes need one initial reload; launcher/reload-host edits and C# DLL edits are outside handler hot reload.

## 2026-11-12 Level 2 Candidate Inventory Bootstrap

- **Cycle-breaking path:** `tools/Build-CanonicalVisualModules.py --inventory-staging <new artifacts path> --capacity-evidence <hashed runtime evidence bundle>` is an isolated candidate-inventory mode. It validates the profile's stored sample rows against the bundle's raw SHA-256-verified reports, requires `measurementContext.capacityEligible=true`, the same named `Game` scene/camera/resolution/mix, the measured-envelope capacity model, the 60-FPS target, and positive non-extrapolated capacity before it creates the staging directory. It also checks every candidate's in-memory LOD0 triangle count against that measured capacity before exporting.
- **Candidate output contract:** The mode replays each canonical module recipe in Blender memory using the shared UV, weighted-normal, vertex-wear, and material-batching path. It writes LOD0 FBXs only under a new ignored `artifacts/` child, then imports each candidate FBX to record actual Blender faces, Unity triangles, mesh/vertex counts, and SHA-256 of the exact file bytes in `candidate-inventory.json`. The manifest pins the profile/evidence hashes, stays `promotionAllowed=false` and `normalBuildAllowed=false`, and never writes the profile, source `.blend`, production FBX/LODs, prefab, or bundle. The staging mode cannot enable the normal build; author the exact demands and hashes into the profile, pass `build_budget_report`, and re-inventory final production FBXs before Unity prefab/bundle work.
- **Current blocker:** The checked-in profile remains uncalibrated and the current Level 4 evidence bundle has `capacityEligible=false` because low/high populations differ (15 versus 35 entities). A read-only call to the new gate rejects it before creating the requested staging directory. Candidate FBX generation and Blender-side import inspection therefore remain unrun until a controlled, hashed runtime bundle is available.
- **Local checks:** `python -m unittest tests.python.test_canonical_module_inventory_staging tests.python.test_model_builder_budget_gate_order tests.python.test_model_budget_policy -v` passes 35 tests. These tests cover pre-write rejection, artifact-only paths, measured per-candidate capacity, candidate count/hash records, and preservation of the normal production-builder route. No generated asset or profile output was changed.

## 2026-11-11 Historical Fixed Face Budgets and Shape-Contact Rules (superseded)

Visible penetration fails review except inside concealed mounts, sealed joints, or articulated interfaces. Cylinders, caps, and rings must serve a named mechanical role; avoid decorative circular armor and repeated concentric stacks.
- **Historical scope/evidence:** No production model, FBX, PBR atlas, prefab, or AssetBundle was regenerated or promoted by that earlier budget/rule update. The geometry contact guidance remains an authoring review rule, not a completed fleet-wide intersection audit or in-game appearance check.

## 2026-11-09 Canonical Two-Level Hardpoint Modules (implementation `d50e5f5`)

- **Supersedes the pre-module census below for current behavior.** Level 1 remains the Blender-authored primary chassis and sole authority for the unit's base silhouette, functional assemblies, rig, pivots, sockets, and PBR surface. Level 2 still applies preset/tint/scale to that chassis and may add explicit visual-only equipment modules at canonical hardpoints; a preset or tint alone never implies new geometry.
- **Runtime contract:** `ContentUnitVisualVariantDefinition.modules[]` contains `{slotId, assetName}`. Valid slots are `ERA`, `APS`, `Sponsons`, and `FuelDrums`; the first/primary variant cannot declare modules, later variants allow one module per slot and at most four. Each asset resolves through the owning unit's `assetBundleId`, mounts beneath its canonical attachment group, and adds no gameplay behavior.
- **Current registered matrix:** the runtime census covers 18 units and 53 variants across `sample-native-expansion` (9 units, 28 variants, 19 Level 2 modules) and `tactical-operations` (9 units, 25 variants, 16 Level 2 modules). The separate `sample-expansion` scaffold has no authored gameplay bundle and is not part of this registered matrix.
- **Authoring output:** `tools/Build-CanonicalVisualModules.ps1` is the complete sequence: pinned Blender 4.5.14 generates the declared Level 2 FBXs and canonical manifest, Material Maker 1.7 strictly bakes the shared four-map PBR sets, and Unity 2021.3.45f2 imports the assets and rebuilds both packs under ignored `artifacts/canonical-two-level/content-packs/`. The command stages matching pack definitions and declared bundles under `artifacts/canonical-two-level/validation-packs/` and writes `build-manifest.json`, which fingerprints all current relevant source files and records each staged bundle hash. `Package-Loader.ps1` rejects changed build inputs or staged outputs. Versioned bundles under `content/packs/*/bundles/` remain snapshots and can be stale after asset edits.
- **Plan completion evidence (local only):** Blender regenerated the 35 module FBXs; Material Maker 1.7 passed strict 2048x2048 generation for all 28 shared PBR sets; Unity reopened both bundles, retained RGBA vertex wear, built 35 script-free prefabs, and labeled 73 referenced assets. The full command staged both packs and ran their validators: 48 references for `sample-native-expansion`, 34 for `tactical-operations`, 0 errors. `Package-Loader.ps1` created `artifacts/packages/RogueCommandExpansionLoader-1.19.0.zip` (175,671,862 bytes; SHA-256 `82F78167B11F424CBE545270AEA190DA4E4A2F51DDFC35290A1B2F7BA64369F2`); ZIP inventory checks confirmed the updated module instructions, fresh sample-native content/bundles, and no BepInEx core, Harmony, or Assembly-CSharp. Level 1 primary chassis blends and their functional hierarchy were untouched. No live variant apply/reset was observed.
- **Deployment:** the Release build passed with 0 warnings and errors. With Rogue Command closed, the latest Release DLL and installed DLL match at SHA-256 `50C8CA598EE98B966FCBAB56459F6CF4D76D437A4C26F6DC9FEDB01FEB9D9F7E`; all seven installed plugin/pack files match the current artifacts. ## 2026-11-09 C# Zero-Allocation Hot-Path & Reflection Elimination Pass

- **Optimization Objectives & Zero Functional Loss:**
  - Targeted the hot execution paths across the runtime engine (Mono x64 / Unity 2021.3.45f2 with non-generational Boehm GC) to eliminate per-frame reflection invocations, prevent value-type heap boxing, and drastically reduce GC allocations with 100% preservation of behavior, contracts, and return values.
- **Key Optimized Subsystems:**
  - `RuntimeVisualDirector.cs`:
    - Evaluates `IsEntityDead()` once per frame in `Update()`; when destroyed or dead, immediately returns after updating health transitions, bypassing expensive updates to locomotion animators, audio spatial anchors, radar sweepers, turret targeting, and weapon recoil feedback.
    - Added direct fast-paths for `_entity.IsDestroyed`, `_entity.DestroyHasBeenStarted`, `CurrentHealth`, and `MaxHealth`.
    - Added thread-static argument buffers (`_singleArgStatusEffect`, `_singleArgCandidate`) and direct `HasActiveStatusEffect` checks to avoid allocating argument arrays on every candidate check and status reaction.
  - `RuntimeUnitMechanicsDirector.cs`:
    - Implemented fast-paths casting target to `EntityController ec`, directly accessing compiled properties (`ec.Position`, `ec.CurrentHealth`, `ec.MaxHealth`, `ec.CurrentArmor`, `ec.MaxArmor`, `ec.CurrentShield`, `ec.MaxShield`, `ec.UniqueEntityId`, `ec.EntityId`, `ec.IsDestroyed`, `ec.DestroyHasBeenStarted`, `ec.IsEnemy`) without reflection or `Vector3`/`int` boxing.
    - In `EnsureReflection()`, cached method parameter counts (`_healParamCount`, `_repairArmorParamCount`, `_chargeShieldParamCount`, `_takeDamageParamCount`) and cached parsed status effect enum objects (`ResolveParsedStatusEffect`, `_cachedDurationUnlimited`, `_cachedDurationSeconds`), eliminating `MethodInfo.GetParameters()` allocations during combat pulses.
  - `RuntimeGameplayCommandDirector.cs`:
    - Direct fast-path for `unit.EntityId` in `ResolveEntityIdString(unit)` before falling back to reflection.
    - Statically cached `_allStatusEffects = (StatusEffect[])Enum.GetValues(typeof(StatusEffect))` and `_cachedTickDurationField`, preventing array allocations inside unit mechanics census loops.
    - Fast-pathed `unit.GetComponent<Visual.RuntimeVisualDirector>()` before allocating `Component[]` arrays via `GetComponents<Component>()`.
    - Direct cast `vd is Visual.RuntimeVisualDirector rvd` in `GetLiveJammedTurretCount` and `GetUnitMechanicsCensus` to read `rvd.IsTurretJammed`, `rvd.CurrentRecoilOffset`, and `rvd.DoorOpenProgress`.
  - `RuntimeHazardManager.cs`:
    - In `UpdateTick()`, added `anyReady` check before gathering entities, saving `new List<EntityController>()` candidate allocations on ~98% of frames where tick intervals are active.
    - Pre-sized `candidates` list and switched to index-based `for` loop.
  - `RuntimeAiBattlefieldCollector.cs`:
    - Pre-sized capacities for `aiSnapshots`, `hostileSnapshots`, and `hazardSnapshots`.
  - `AgentDiagnosticsFormatter.cs`:
    - Pre-sized `StringBuilder` in `Serialize(view)` to prevent repeated buffer re-allocations on large telemetry payloads.
    - Added `WriteList` using index-based `for` loops on `IList` (lists and arrays) to eliminate `IEnumerator` allocations, and optimized `WriteString` with index-based loop.
- **Verification & Parity:**
  - Release build: 0 warnings, 0 errors.
  - C# validation harness: 558/559 passing (1 Windows symlink skip, 0 failures).
  - Python test suite: 479/479 passing (1 skip).
  - Registry validator: 4/4 manifests clean.

## 2026-11-08 Phase 2 Core Harmony CIL Transpilers (16 Transpilers Total, 65 Hooks)

- **Implementation commit:** local Release build verified, DLL deployed to Steam with SHA-256 `3DF709EBCAF940789E5BD6A6E9E817D611AB634C240E27BDBA4C727DF768D54A`.
- **Core CIL Transpiler Suite Expansion (7 new transpilers, 16 total):**
  - `EntityFactory.CreateBuildingPlacementGhost`: value-returning (`GameObject`) method. Stack duplication pattern via `OpCodes.Dup` before `Ret`, followed by `ldarg.0`, `ldarg.1`, calling `OnCreateBuildingPlacementGhostTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `placementGhostTranspilerCount`.
  - `EntityController.Init`: intercepts unit controller initialization in `EntityController.Init`. Injects `ldarg.0`, `ldarg.1` before every `Ret` exit with `TransferLabels`, calling `OnEntityInitTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `entityInitTranspilerCount`.
  - `EntityController.Destroy`: entry-point interception (index 0) with `TransferLabels`, passing `ldarg.0..2` to `OnEntityDestroyTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `entityDestroyTranspilerCount`.
  - `EntityController.ExecuteCommand`: entry-point interception (index 0) with `TransferLabels`, passing `ldarg.0` to `OnEntityExecuteCommandTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `commandExecutedTranspilerCount`.
  - `EntityController.ExecuteNextCommand`: step advancement in command execution loop before `Ret` with `TransferLabels`, calling `OnEntityExecuteNextCommandTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `commandNextStepTranspilerCount`.
  - `EntityController.ResetCurrentCommand`: command reset callback before `Ret` with `TransferLabels`, calling `OnEntityResetCurrentCommandTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `commandResetTranspilerCount`.
  - `EntityController.DeleteCommandChain`: command queue purge callback before `Ret` with `TransferLabels`, calling `OnEntityDeleteCommandChainTranspilerHook`. Telemetry counter: `commandChainDeletedTranspilerCount`.
- **CIL Mono/netstandard2.1 Compatibility (CS7069 Prevention):** Zero direct references to `[mscorlib]Label` or `ILGenerator` in netstandard2.1 plugins. Fail-safe `try/catch` wrapping around each individual transpiler.
- **Telemetry & MCP Bridge:** 65 total installed Harmony patches; 28 atomic simulation counters in `RuntimeHooks.cs` (including 16 transpiler counters), fully serialized in `AgentDiagnosticsFormatter.SerializeRuntimeHooksState` and queryable via `diagnostics_get_runtime_hooks`.
- **Local Validation:** Release plugin and test builds: 0 warnings, 0 errors. C# validation harness: 558/559 passing (1 Windows symlink skip, 0 failures). Python suite: 479/479 passing (1 skip). Registry validator: 4/4 manifests clean.

- **Implementation commit:** `56bc4b5a7f8eaa33b70eb8b0e067c837cf7848ea`, pushed to origin/codex/rogue-unit-balance-20261018.
- **Dynamic Tactical Retreat & Regroup Geometry (`AiSafeVectorPolicy.cs`, `AiBattlefieldBlackboard.cs`):**

## 2026-11-08 synthetic stage-jump authorization (runtime validation automation)

- User decision (this session): explicit authorization to falsify game state for
  runtime-validation automation, implemented as a new sanctioned diagnostics seam:
  `diagnostics_jump_to_level` → `LoaderDiagnosticsComponent.TryJumpToLevelJson` →
  `RuntimeIntermissionSyntheticJump.TryJumpToLevel` (reflection on `StageMap.JumpTo`,
  fail-closed). It mutates the active run WITHOUT touching profile/save files and is
  valid only on the isolated QA profile (Profile2) under the checkpoint discipline
  (every profile file hashed before the game opens; selector+hashes verified exact
  match after it closes).
- Anti-confusion rules: every result carries `kind="syntheticJumpResult"` and
  `synthetic=true`, and the before/after stage/level. Such a call is never evidence
  of natural map progression or "abrir la primera oleada"; entering a combat scene
  after a synthetic jump only proves the transition through the seam executed without
  throwing (verify the transition with `diagnostics_get_intermission_census`).
- The `TryEnterLevelNodeWorkflow` (deterministic native-node picker) remains the
  FIRST choice for level entry; the synthetic jump is only for when the native map
  reports no choosable node (e.g. `ChosenPath` fully filled with
  `IsCurrentLevelFinished=True`, where the LaunchButton also stays inactive and no
  real click can advance the run).
- Note: the equivalent rule change to `AGENTS.md` was attempted and blocked by the
  protected-file guard (approval timed out; silence is not consent). That edit was
  not retried and no other path (terminal, execute_code) was used to bypass it. The
  authorization is recorded here as session documentation instead. The pinned-assembly
  method name `JumpTo` was verified from the `.NET metadata strings heap` of
  `Assembly-CSharp.dll` (SHA-256 `d2da384df3318e7ac52db58eb13699ec483790b469effa46205de716825e543b`);
  overloads are probed in order `(bool,int)` then `(int)` and reported in the result.
- **Second seam** (`diagnostics_load_next_level` → `TryLoadNextLevelJson` →
  `RuntimeIntermissionSyntheticJump.TryStartNextLevelLoad`): invokes the native
  `LoadScene.StartCoroutine("LoadNextLevel")` via reflection. Neither `JumpTo(int)`
  nor this coroutine transitions the scene by themselves (observed ~24s without
  transition). The load that worked once in live Steam (artifact `c75d54d6`,
  evidence `artifacts/runtime-smoke/ui-click-20261108g/level4-entry-evidence.json`)
  is: `component/call_method LoadLevel` on the LoadNextLevel GO (928974) after the
  jump + coroutine, then the native level opener `LoadingHider` appears and its
  `INICIAR` Unity UI button (GO `Button` 1008136, NOT the Michsky map LaunchButton)
  advances to gameplay.
- **Non-determinism (do not loop)**: identical `Jump → coroutine → LoadLevel` runs
  transitioned in 1 of ~5 fresh-process attempts; others stall on the loading
  screen ("Cargando...", empty bar, spinner) with `LoadingHider` never populated and
  the census stuck at stage=4 level=-1. No error is logged anywhere (BepInEx and
  Player logs clean). Next diagnosis requires the full precondition matrix
  (which method reads what state), not blind retries.
- **MCP tools quirks observed 2026-11-08**: use full component names
  (`RogueCommandExpansionLoader.Runtime.LoaderDiagnosticsComponent`, not short
  `LoaderDiagnosticsComponent`) in `component/call_method` (short name → HTTP 500);
  the `LoaderDiagnostics` GameObject id changes per scene — re-resolve via
  `find_objects_with_component` after every scene change (`-4880` works in
  MainMenu, `-67812` in Intermission) or calls return HTTP 500; the diagnostics GO
  components live on a DontDestroyOnLoad object but under a scene-specific
  component copy; `arguments` param shape differences include some tools writing
  `"button has no id"` when the search envelope lacks an id key — resolve the id
  via `search_gameobjects` first, then `TryUiClickJson("[name, id]")` directly;
  the bridge takes `args` as a JSON string inside an `args=` query param, not a JSON
  body.

## 2026-11-04 locomotion animation follow-up

## 2026-11-05 standalone visual census optimization

- **Scope:** The four independent visual censuses (`RuntimeVisualQualityCensus`, `RuntimeVisualSurfaceCensus`, `RuntimeVisualMaterialCensus`, and `RuntimeSocketAuditCensus`) now share `RuntimeVisualCensusSupport` for roster collection, identity accessors, bounded hierarchy traversal, mesh/material helpers, and deduplicated diagnostic names.
- **Performance contract:** Each census reads player/AI registries once, deduplicates entity references, clamps rows to 256, visits at most 4,096 hierarchy nodes, caps renderer components at 256, material slots at 512, diagnostic names at 256, mesh color inspections at 32, and color samples at 4,096. Disabled renderers remain visible in renderer/hierarchy counts but do not contribute material, texture, or mesh telemetry. `sharedMaterials` is used without the material-instancing fallback, and texture maps require `Material.HasProperty`. The material census caches binding metadata by material reference for the duration of one sweep, avoiding repeated shader-keyword/property/texture reflection for shared native materials; non-loader-owned native rows keep renderer/material-slot counts but skip detailed binding names/maps and expose partial-coverage flags.
- **Partial-result contract:** Quality, surface, material, and socket rows expose hierarchy/detail sampling flags. Socket canonical-prefix semantics remain unchanged; no catalog semantic migration was introduced. `LoaderDiagnosticsComponent` now accepts `maxTrackedUnits` for surface, material, and socket getters; MCP snapshot/tool forwarding passes `[64]`.
- **Getter fan-out:** Same-frame scalar reads for visual render and visual-quality properties reuse one bounded census per frame, avoiding repeated Unity main-thread sweeps while preserving per-frame freshness.
- **TDD/local validation:** RED was observed for duplicated player/AI roster entries, disabled-renderer standalone work, the 33-mesh sampling bound, repeated shared-material binding inspection, and native-detail skipping; GREEN passed the focused `RuntimeAndDiagnostics` suite with `171/171`. Fresh Release builds completed with 0 warnings/errors; the exact full harness passed `495/496` with one Windows symlink-privilege skip. Python bridge suite passed `26/26`; the full Python suite passed `190` with one platform skip.
- **Evidence boundary:** Session `artifacts/runtime-smoke/visual-censuses-native-skip-20261105-143429/` used the verified 1.0.9/Unity/Assembly-CSharp identity, Profile2, a paused live Game scene, and final loader SHA-256 `9f695a62af3bc0897db9f260c9570baaefc39fbee4df5923fbd27451b565ad6f`. The four direct standalone getters returned 12 rows each with zero omissions: surface `2283.18ms`, quality `1664.96ms`, material `1455.82ms`, socket `570.46ms`; the bounded four-getter snapshot returned `successCount=4/errorCount=0` in `3307.63ms`. Native material rows deliberately report renderer/material-slot counts with `materialSamplingBounded=true` and `nameSamplingBounded=true`; loader-owned Arachnid Hexapod retained 46 material names without sampling flags. This is direct Steam runtime evidence for endpoint execution and bounded partial-result semantics only; natural offer, production, AI selection, and emergent combat behavior remain untested. Profile verification allowed only the authorized `Profile2/metaSavegame.dat` delta; the final loader remained deployed and hash-equal.

- **Assembly-CSharp.dll MVID**: `db0e9462-128d-4296-9900-de18b4bd0c00`

Keep the version/SHA/MVID check above as the compatibility identity before relying on reflected signatures or metadata tokens.
- **Unity CoreModule SHA-256**: `6305c82c17ffe111a016e8d8ca3e8b2d9203980b4f58cb6e90171e5629994290` (MVID `37d484f4-070f-4518-bfa9-9fa57b7a2871`)
- **Render Pipeline**: Universal Render Pipeline (URP, `UniversalRenderPipelineAsset`, PathID 2768 in `globalgamemanagers.assets`). Shipped vehicles render via untextured submeshes with `Shader Graphs/EntityColorShader`. Modded AssetBundles must embed self-contained compiled shaders (`RogueCommand/VehiclePBR_VertexWear` or `Standard`).

## 2. Steam Bridge & Runtime Verification State

### BepInEx MCP Bridge Protocol 2.0
- **Process Discovery Pitfall**: The executable is named `Rogue Command.exe` (with a space). Standard `Get-Process -Name RogueCommand` fails. Always query via exact CimInstance:
  ```powershell
  Get-CimInstance Win32_Process | Where-Object { $_.Name -like "*Rogue*Command*" }
  ```
- **Lifecycle & Health**: The in-process game bridge is `http://127.0.0.1:8080/mcp/` (confirmed from `ModdersHelperApp.py` and a live process); the companion MCP transport is a separate service/port. A ping failure when no game process runs is expected. Follow pings with at least one root GameObject or scene read before asserting connection. Consult [STEAM-RUNTIME-CONTROL.md](STEAM-RUNTIME-CONTROL.md).
- **Bridge Query Schema**: The bridge accepts JSON-RPC `unity_query` payloads (`{"query": "FindObjectsOfType", "type": "EntityController"}`) returning serialized hierarchy state, component values, and active scene tags without game engine stall.
- **Verified Startup State**: Steam launch (AppID `1461910`) loads `RogueCommandExpansionLoader.dll` with 26 verified Harmony hooks, registers both sample packs (`sample-native:expansion` and `sample-reactive-ai:expansion`), and transitions cleanly across `MainMenu`, `RunSetup`, `IntermissionScreen`, and `Game`.
- **First-run tutorial start (2026-10-18)**: The user's screenshot showed the `FirstRun` tutorial's `INICIAR` screen. The in-game button's persistent listener resolved to `PlaceMapObjectsBeforePlayerCanStart.OnPlayerHasPressedStart`; it was invoked once through the native Unity `Button`, and `_playerHasNotPressedStartYet` changed to `false`. The temporary button listener inspector was removed and the bridge had no temporary watchers or patches afterward.
- **Pause verification correction (2026-10-18)**: `InGameMenu._isShowing=true` means only that the menu overlay is visible. In the active tutorial, the HUD clock advanced from `00:16` to `01:19` with `Time.timeScale=1`. A fresh runtime probe then read `Time.timeScale=0` and `Time.time=537.5239`; 16.38 seconds later, `Time.time` was still `537.5239` while `Time.unscaledTime` advanced, and background `PrintWindow` captures showed the HUD at `03:51`. Static inspection of the exact `1.0.9` assembly identity located `Navigator.Pause()`/`Unpause()` and the `_isPaused` state. `Navigator.Pause()` was invoked once through the bridge-side reflection helper; it reported `_isPaused=true` and scale `0` before and after. Then `RclRuntimeTimeControl` was removed without calling its resume method. A separate read-only probe, with no pause helper attached, confirmed `_isPaused=true`, `Time.timeScale=0`, and `Time.time=537.5239`; the probe and action were then removed, and the final component list had no `Rcl*` components. The game remains open in `Game` with the menu visible, tutorial `7/26`, and natively paused. The user confirmed the HUD clock advances when resuming. The no-focus capture is `%TEMP%\rogue-command-native-pause-verified.png`. Reusable steps and `tools/Capture-RogueCommand-Background.ps1` are in [STEAM-RUNTIME-CONTROL.md](STEAM-RUNTIME-CONTROL.md), including the screenshot fallback after repeated failed or contradictory bridge attempts.

### Profile Isolation & Cleanup Invariants
- **Profile Backups**: Before a live run, resolve the actual selector and hash all user-profile files. For acceptance that skips `FirstRun`, use only a dedicated isolated QA profile with no saved run, created or selected through the game's native profile-management flow. Never complete tutorial flags on the user's profile. Restore the original selector after the game exits and verify the user's profile hashes are unchanged. If isolation cannot be verified, stop without bypassing the tutorial. Historical sessions that completed tutorial flags on Profile 1 describe past state only and are not a procedure to repeat.
- **Steam Cloud Safeguard**: Steam Cloud is disabled by persistent user instruction. Preserve that setting during tests and cleanup; never toggle it.
- **Package Deployment Integrity**: Distributable ZIPs (`artifacts/packages/RogueCommandExpansionLoader-0.1.0.zip`) contain 9 entries: loader DLL, schemas, and content packs. Never include BepInEx core, Harmony, game assemblies, PDBs, or Unity `Library/` outputs in packages.

---

## 3. Core Architectural Invariants

### A. Production Building Invariant (1:1 Factory Mapping)
Every added unit must declare its own namespaced `productionBuilding`.
- The loader registers an additive factory row and dormant prefab clone, mapping that factory to exactly one produced unit.
- Native factory mappings are strictly preserved.
- The stage offers the factory blueprint in randomized reward pools; player purchase and placement produce the unit.
- Stage `unitIds` and AI `unitEntries` reference the produced unit ID.
- Nine product/building pairs are currently declared in `sample-native-expansion`:
  - `sample-native:scout-tank` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:scout-tank-factory`
  - `sample-native:heavy-artillery` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:heavy-artillery-factory`
  - `sample-native:assault-walker` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:assault-walker-factory`
  - `sample-native:atlas-crane-mech` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:atlas-crane-mech-factory`
  - `sample-native:bastion-siege-mech` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:bastion-siege-mech-factory`
  - `sample-native:arachnid-hexapod` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:arachnid-hexapod-factory`
  - `sample-native:overlord-command-hq` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:overlord-command-hq-factory`
  - `sample-native:skirmish-bike` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:skirmish-bike-factory`
  - `sample-native:surge-tank` $\leftrightarrow$ `sample-native:surge-tank-factory`

**Structured unit fields:** All existing `ContentUnitDefinition` sub-definitions are schema-backed and package-validated, but this does not imply runtime effects. Positive finite `movement.moveSpeed` and validated `customBalance` values are applied to the incoming `EntityBalancingParameters` row before registration, not written as final values into native caches. `customBalance` remains `double` from JSON DTO through validation, mapper, and provider so JSON integers retain exact parsing precision; integer-backed `ChangeableValue` values are capped at `16,777,216` because native `CalculateResultingIntValue` widens the row `Int32` to `Single` before applying deltas and rounding. The schema and raw `balanceOverrides` validator enforce the same integer bound; float-backed values remain capped at `Single.MaxValue`. The explicit map covers 25 per-unit `ChangeableValue` members with verified row fields; `Attack1Cooldown` maps to `attackCooldown`, `SpawnTtl` to `duration2`, and global-only `ManaRechargePerSecond` is excluded. Precedence is `customBalance` over raw `balanceOverrides`, then structured movement. Native cache invalidation can rebuild from the row and preserve `CardChangeScriptableObject` modifiers. The earlier 2026-10-21 MainMenu MCP smoke exercised an interim cache-seeding build only; it does not validate this row-based path, a spawned unit, or gameplay consumption. Keep other unverified fields (weapons, armor, healing, stealth, aura, gathering, AI behavior, and others) unmapped until a direct native/runtime contract is established; never use arbitrary DTO names as reflection targets.

### B. Stage-Scoped Blueprint Reward Pools & Roguelike Reward Mechanics
- **Roguelike Reward Selection**: In `ChooseCard.PresentCardsToChoose`, pool-backed rewards query `BlueprintPool.BlueprintsInPool`. The game removes owned/shown cards, seeds `SeededRandom`, and picks available candidates.
- **Pool Scoping**: The loader clones `BlueprintPoolList` and referenced `BlueprintPool` objects only when a declared stage lists custom units (`RCL_POOL_<sha256>`). Custom units appear only in pools scoped to the active stage; native and other stage pools reject them.
- **Filter Preservation**: Native per-pool filters (`blueprintFilter.IsBlueprintValid`) are preserved in cloned pools.
- **Starting Blueprints vs Regular Blueprints**: `isAllowedAsStartingBlueprint` sets the dedicated starting-deck flag on the unit row via `EntityBalancingStore.IsAllowedAsStartingBlueprint`; regular blueprints require stage pool inclusion.
- **Generic Fallback Scoping**: The no-pool reward fallback in `ChooseCard.PresentCardsToChoose` is scoped via `Game.StageMap.CurrentStage`. Only loader-owned factories declared by the active stage are eligible; unrelated mod factories are excluded.

### C. Additive AI Unit Rosters & Command Lifecycle
- **Additive Roster Structure**: `ContentAiProfileDefinition.unitEntries` and `productionBuildingEntries` add namespaced units and factories to `AiBase.entitiesInAiDeck`.
- **Capacity Enforcement**: `ScheduledBaseBuildAiModule` rereads active deck `MaxLiving` before each attempt, counts distinct living AI buildings via `ExistingControllers.AiBuildings()`, and respects capacity bounds.
- **Command Lifecycle Tags**: Harvester Raider (`sample:harvester-raider`) and Siege Assault (`sample:siege-assault`) use active command tags as truth: units executing that module's attack-move are skipped, becoming eligible again when their command tag clears.
- **Harvester Raider Module**: Locates player crystal harvesters (`ExistingControllers.Instance.Harvesters`), selects idle combat units, and dispatches attack-move harassment squads.
- **Siege Assault Module**: Scans discovered player structures (`PlayerRefineries`, `EngineeringHq`), gathers combat squads, and dispatches coordinated strike waves by priority (`ClosestBuilding`, `Refinery`, `Hq`).
- **Reactive Base Defense**: Scans attackers targeting AI base buildings within `defenseRadiusCells` (default 12 cells) and dispatches mobile defenders before weapon firing range.
- **Patrol and Intercept (`sample:patrol-and-intercept`)**: The default `patrolMode=AttackMove` preserves tagged attack-move waypoint orders and interception. Optional `patrolMode=Native` clears and seeds the native patrol positions with up to eight nearest distinct in-radius AI-building positions, then issues tagged `EntityCommand.Patrol(0)` only when the current command is `DoNothing` and the private native `_commandChain` is verified empty. The pinned field is read reflectively with an exact `LinkedList<EntityCommand>` type check; missing/incompatible/unreadable queue state suppresses native dispatch. This protects queued `Move` commands hidden behind a current `DoNothing`, since static inspection shows Patrol cycles forward/backward without returning to queued commands. Intercepts remain attack-move orders, run first, and share the `maxPatrolUnits` per-scan cap. The local harness exercises queue preservation as well as parser/default behavior, route selection/capping, native order dispatch, idle-only selection, and intercept preservation using a value-type `EntityCommand` fixture. Detailed signatures, CIL evidence and runtime boundary: [AI-NATIVE-PATROL.md](AI-NATIVE-PATROL.md). No Steam match has validated reflection against the shipped assembly, pathing, route motion, or command interruption/resumption.

### D. Engine Hooks, Serialization & TextMeshPro Fallback
- **TextMeshPro In-Memory Fallback**: Rogue Command 1.0.9 does not ship with a serialized `TMP Settings.asset` in `Resources/`. Without a valid instance, TextMeshPro crashes when layout components call `TMP_Settings.defaultStyleSheet`, `linebreakingRules`, and `fallbackFontAssets`. The loader hooks `Resources.Load(string, Type)` for `"TMP Settings"`, creating a dynamic `TMP_Settings` ScriptableObject with initialized `m_defaultStyleSheet` (refreshing `"Normal"` style), `m_linebreakingRules` (dictionaries initialized), empty non-null `fallbackFontAssets`, and assigns static `s_Instance` to eliminate layout crashes.
- **Shared `Resources.Load` Overload Resolution**: On Unity CoreModule (`6305c82c17ffe111a016e8d8ca3e8b2d9203980b4f58cb6e90171e5629994290` / `37d484f4-070f-4518-bfa9-9fa57b7a2871`), generic `Resources.Load<T>(string)` calls `Resources.Load(string, Type)`. Harmony hooks resolve only that single overload; custom assets return only when matching the requested `Type`, otherwise delegating to Unity's original loader.
- **Stage Node Distribution Overrides**: `ContentStageParameterOverride` validates and sets boolean fields on `StageParametersScriptableObject`, including `fillFirstLevelWithCardRewards`, `putDefaultCardRewardInCenter`, and `fillLastLevelWithShops`, plus branching and reward weight floats.
- **Structured Stage Start Range**: `startCountRange` is a native `Vector2Int` read by `LevelProgression.CreateLevelMap` for initial map-width selection. The loader accepts an invariant-culture `"minimum,maximum"` pair (ordered 1–64) through a dedicated parser, then checks its maximum against the final `maxWidth`; this struct is not handled by the generic scalar override path. Unit tests cover parser bounds and the width predicate, but not live stage generation.
- **Diagnostic Trace Bounds & Loss Counters**: Bounded diagnostic traces (`MaxTraceCharacters = 4096`) place loss/omission counters (`omittedIdObservations`, `omittedPoolQueries`) immediately after `observedSources` before unbounded detail collections (`offeredProducts`, `globalFactories`, `stageFactories`, `pools`), ensuring tail truncation never drops loss diagnostics.
- **Serialized Scene Bootstrap Flow**: In `level0`, `ImageLOGO` (PathID 3) has `ImageFading` (PathID 66) whose `onFadeInEnd` triggers `LoadScene.LoadMainMenu` on `Loader` (PathID 5), transitioning cleanly from `EmptyBootUpScene` to `MainMenu`.

### E. Persistent Profile Unlock Progression & Savegame Architecture
- **Savegame Progression Store**: Rogue Command tracks unlocked blueprints, mutators, and cards persistently in `Profiles/Profile<n>/savegame.dat` via `ProfileUnlockedCardStore` (token `0x060004ad`).
- **Blueprint Unlock Verification**: In `ChooseCard.PresentCardsToChoose`, native cards query `ProfileUnlocks.IsBlueprintUnlocked(string)` before inclusion in regular pools.
- **Modded Blueprints Invariant**: Expansion units must not corrupt or irreversibly overwrite `savegame.dat`. When `isAllowedAsStartingBlueprint` is true, the factory blueprint is granted to the player's starting selection without mutating persistent profile unlock arrays.
- **Profile Isolation Protocol**: For acceptance that bypasses `FirstRun`, create/select a dedicated isolated QA profile through the native profile-management flow; never apply `Game.TutorialState` completion to the user's profile. Verify the actual selector, QA profile isolation, and absence of a saved run before testing. After the game exits, restore the original selector and confirm user-profile file hashes are unchanged. See [STEAM-RUNTIME-CONTROL.md](STEAM-RUNTIME-CONTROL.md); if isolation cannot be established, do not bypass the tutorial.

## 5. Content Expansion Roster

> **Supersession note (2026-10-24):** The roster and authored-asset counts below are a historical snapshot, not the current manifest. `content/packs/sample-native-expansion/content.json` now declares 9 native-template products, with 9 product visual overlays, 2 factory visual overlays, and 7 custom product cards (Arachnid and Overlord retain native cards). `docs/research/LEGACY-UNITS-LOADER-1.19-AUDIT.md` is the current audit; these static declarations and bundle checks do not establish gameplay or rendering in a Steam match.

### 5 Custom Units (`sample-native-expansion`)
All units provide English (`en-US`) and Spanish (`es-XL`) localizations, custom models, and card Sprites:
1. `scout-tank` (Sandlance Scout): Fast recon flanker, cost 70, HP 130, speed 14.5, sight 8. Template: `PCXBaseTank`. Factory: `scout-tank-factory`, Card: `Sprites/ScoutTankCard`.
2. `heavy-artillery` (Colossus Siege): Long-range siege bombarder, cost 300, HP 160, speed 9.0, range 16, cooldown 4.0, AoE 1.5, armor 2. Template: `ArtilleryTank`. Factory: `heavy-artillery-factory`, Card: `Sprites/HeavyArtilleryCard`.
3. `assault-walker` (Ironclad Walker): Heavy anti-chaff walker, cost 380, HP 420, speed 12.0, damage 4, cooldown 0.4, range 6, armor 1.0. Template: `GatlingWalker`. Factory: `assault-walker-factory`, Card: `Sprites/AssaultWalkerCard`.
4. `skirmish-bike` (Skirmish Strike Bike): High-mobility raider, cost 55, HP 75, speed 24.0, damage 32, AoE 1.8. Template: `BombBike`. Factory: `skirmish-bike-factory`, Card: `Sprites/SkirmishBikeCard`.
5. `surge-tank` (Surge Arc Tank): Electromagnetic disruption tank, cost 210, HP 150, speed 13.0, damage 12, range 4.5, cooldown 1.8, armor 2. Template: `LightningTank`. Factory: `surge-tank-factory`, Card: `Sprites/SurgeTankCard`.

### Factory Blueprint and Visual Scaffolding Rules
- Factory prefabs must provide standard 1:1 footprint bounds ($3.0\text{m} \times 3.0\text{m}$) with `Socket_Deploy_Unit` transform positioned at the door threshold.
- Units instantiate dormant at origin until factory build cycle finishes, triggering rollout slide animation.

### 3 Progressive Stages & AI Profiles
- **Stages**: `training-variant` (8 levels, reward 160), `fortified-frontline` (10 levels, reward 240, starting crystal 80), `core-battleground` (12 levels, reward 350, crystal 150).
- **AI Profiles**: `quick-hunter-extension` (rapid build interval 8s), `horde-swarm-assault` (swarm interval 12s), `raider-patrol-strike` (patrol strike interval 10s).
- **Reactive AI Pack (`sample-reactive-ai`)**: `sample:reactive-base-defense` module intercepting attackers on AI bases within `defenseRadiusCells` (default 12 cells).

For texture work, use [PBR-TEXTURE-WORKFLOW.md](PBR-TEXTURE-WORKFLOW.md) as the shared handoff. Blender 4.5.14 remains the reproducible atlas baseline; Material Maker 1.7 is the required local authoring pass and is run headlessly by `tools/Run-MaterialMakerBake.ps1` with a temporary export target and rendering-device workaround, while ArmorPaint remains optional when its official binary is available to the user. External texture tools export images only, while Unity's existing builder remains responsible for importer settings and prefab material wiring. Prepare local copies with `tools/Setup-TextureAuthoringTools.ps1 -MaterialMaker`.

### Optional Hardflow Vehicle Modeling

- Hardflow 1.23.0 is staged from its official release ZIP with SHA-256 `5f4b2fe1eddfc3b4d0397d8052a8fbdfe8bf8f4292c0b8c6ff79bee00349b947` by `tools/Setup-Hardflow.ps1`. It installs into a project-local Blender extension profile under ignored `.tools/`; launch it with `tools/Start-BlenderWithHardflow.ps1` to keep the user's regular Blender preferences unchanged.
- Use it for hand-authored hard-surface vehicle geometry such as armor cutouts, vents, bolt circles, welded seams, and routed cables. The primary Blender source, generated FBX, UVs, vertex wear, sockets, model budgets, and Material Maker texture contract remain authoritative. Hardflow is optional and is not imported by headless builders or pack validators.
- The upstream manifest declares Blender 4.2+; the project is pinned to Blender 4.5.14 and setup verifies activation there. Extension availability/mesh smoke checks are local authoring evidence, not in-game render evidence.
- Local Blender 4.5.14 validation installed Hardflow, repeated setup successfully, rejected an incorrect expected hash without altering the archive, and visually applied a rectangular boolean cut plus six vent slots to the temporary `TEMP_HARDFLOW_TEST_PLATE` in `artifacts/hardflow-smoke-20261110/HardflowSmoke.blend`. The saved single mesh has 68 vertices, 41 faces, no modifiers, and no non-manifold edges. This does not establish in-game appearance.

### Domain Technical Details

#### 1. 3D Model Authoring & BMesh Vertex Shading
- **Topological BMesh Vertex Wear**: Dihedral angle analysis ($\ge 35^\circ$) and normal convexity projection ($\Delta = (v_{other} - v_{edge}) \cdot \vec{n}_{f1}$) maps bright steel wear $G \in [0.7, 1.0]$ to convex edges and cavity AO $R \in [0.25, 0.40]$ to concave seams.
- **Soot Shading**: Channel B defaulted to $0.0$ (clean metal), scaling to $1.0$ at muzzles ($R = 0.60\text{m}$) and exhausts ($R = 0.45\text{m}$) with quadratic dropoff $(1 - d/R)^2$.
- **Procedural Enhancements**: Parametric catenary track sag curve $z(t) = height - s \cdot \sin(\pi \cdot t)$ (`--track-sag`), procedural BMesh faceted armor paneling (`--armor-plating`), and automated 2-bone IK solver constraints on `Bone_Shin_*` (`--ik-rig`).
- **Weapon & Preset Roster**: 16 CLI presets (including `beam_destroyer` and `vls_missile_cruiser`) and 11 procedural weapon assemblies (featuring `laser_beam` and `vls_missiles`).
- **Multi-System Sockets**: Sockets use canonical names with nested `FirePoint_0` under `Socket_MuzzlePrimary`, `Socket_EngineAudio`, `Socket_WeaponAudio`, and `Socket_FootContact_*` at $Z = 0.0$.
- Detailed architecture: [3D-MODEL-AUTHORING-ENHANCEMENTS.md](3D-MODEL-AUTHORING-ENHANCEMENTS.md).

#### 2. Mech & Walker Rigging
- Articulated skeletal armatures for bipedal walkers (8 bones) and quadrupeds (10 bones).
- Rigid vertex weighting ($1.0$ per armor plate) assigned on unbatched source meshes before `batch_and_join_by_material`.
- **In-Place Invariant**: $\Delta\text{pos} = (0, 0, 0)$ on `Bone_Root` across all frames of `Action_Walk`.
- Bakes 5 action clips (`Action_Idle`, `Action_Walk`, `Action_Drive`, `Action_FireRecoil`, `Action_Death`) wired to Generic Mecanim Animators.

#### 3. Combat Balance & Tactical Simulation
- `tools/simulate_combat_balance.py` provides discrete 1v1 and squad matchups over the native entity table and expansion units; row counts vary by input snapshot. The CEI is `sqrt(DPS * BaselineEHP) / (CrystalCost + 0.3 * ProductionDuration) * 100`.
- The skill calibration matrix targets CEI bands: Scout 30–45, Main Battle Tank 12–20, Siege 6–16, and Mech 10–18.
- Historical calibration-matrix snapshot: Ironclad Walker armor was calibrated to 1.0 (CEI 14.9), and Colossus cost to 300 (CEI 15.8). CEI depends on its input snapshot; the current 2026-10-22 run reports Colossus CEI 16.0 below.
- Historical 2026-10-18 simulation reported Sandlance CEI 39.8 (inside the 30–45 scout band); a 100-trial squad simulation of two Scouts vs one `PCXBaseTank` reported 100% Scout wins and raised `ASYMMETRIC_SUPERIORITY`. A separate 2026-10-21 run recorded Sandlance CEI 97.6 and a simulated win vs `PCXBaseTank` in 3.85s with 78.5% HP remaining; that is an earlier input snapshot, not the current result. The current 2026-10-22 `--all-mod-units` run reports CEI 36.9 and a simulated victory in 26.35s with 3.1% HP remaining. These are model outputs, not live balance evidence: nominal DPS/CEI includes `Damage2`/`Attack2Cooldown` when supplied, but timed duel and squad events execute only primary `Damage1` volleys and do not reproduce full delivery or live target-selection behavior. Compare results only with their matching input snapshot.

#### 4. Mutators, Relics & Battlefield Hazards
- Integrates with native `CardChangeScriptableObject` targeting the 26 canonical `ChangeableValue` attributes:
  `Cost`, `ProductionDuration`, `MaxCapacity`, `MaxHealth`, `MaxShield`, `MaxMana`, `MaxRank`, `MoveSpeed`, `SightRadius`, `Damage1`, `Damage2`, `WeaponRange`, `Attack1Cooldown`, `Attack2Cooldown`, `EffectRadius1`, `EffectRadius2`, `SkillRange`, `HealAmount1`, `HealAmount2`, `GainCreditsAmount`, `SkillManaCost`, `Duration1`, `SpawnTtl`, `ManaRechargePerSecond`, `MaxArmor`, `ArmorProtection`. The content-pack relic schema currently accepts `Health` as an alias for `MaxHealth` only.
- Relics use `RelicBalancingStore`; stage-start card changes use `ManageStartCardChanges`. `multiplyValueWithMetaProgressionMultiplier` is a string key. The content-pack relic contract rejects Heat scaling until its per-level balance limit is validated. Environmental hazards use trigger colliders (`isTrigger = true`) with amortized tick rates ($\ge 0.5\text{s}$). When authoring supported dynamic Heat changes, keep major combat stats at $\le 3\%$ per level and `AttackRange` at $+15\%$ maximum.

#### 5. VFX & Audio Authoring
- URP ParticleSystem prefabs use authored alpha materials where present. `RuntimeVisualVfxFallback` searches URP particle shaders first, then older/mobile and sprite fallbacks, and shares the fallback material across low-count emitters.
- Spatial 3D audio anchored to `Socket_EngineAudio` and `Socket_WeaponAudio` with logarithmic roll-off (min 5m, max 45m).

## 7. Steam Runtime Smoke & Control Procedures

When verifying loader or content behavior in a live Steam session:
1. **Preflight Health Check**: First locate `Rogue Command.exe` via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, then ping the BepInEx MCP bridge via protocol 2.0. The bridge is in-process: no game process means no live bridge endpoint, so launch through Steam before diagnosing a ping failure. If the process exists and ping still fails, inspect the installed bridge files/log and repair the deployment; do not stop after reporting that it failed.
  2. **Profile Isolation**: For acceptance that bypasses `FirstRun`, record the original selector and hashes for every file in every user profile, then create/select a dedicated QA profile through the native profile-management flow without starting a run. Verify the actual selector under `Application.persistentDataPath` points to QA, QA has no saved run, and all user-profile hashes remain unchanged. Never clone a user profile or apply tutorial completion to the user's profile. If QA cannot be isolated safely, stop without bypassing the tutorial.
3. **Steam Cloud Safeguard**: Preserve the user's existing Steam Cloud preference. For this project it is disabled by persistent user instruction; do not toggle it during a test.
4. **Reversible Deployment**: Back up the eight runtime files in the table below and `LogOutput.log` before deploying new plugin or bundle builds. The ZIP also contains a root-level `INSTALL.txt`; it is package documentation and is not copied into the game for a controlled smoke test.
  5. **Post-Run Restoration**: Close the game normally and confirm its process has exited; verify and restore all eight runtime files to their original SHA-256 hashes and restore the original BepInEx log. Restore the original profile selector and verify every user-profile hash still matches its pre-test value. Preserve QA-created data; never restore or alter user-profile files as a substitute for isolation. Never toggle Steam Cloud as cleanup.
6. **Repeated UI Failures**: After repeated failed inputs or transitions, stop retrying the same action. Use the user's screenshot skill to capture the current desktop/game to the temp directory, inspect the visible scene, focus, dialogs, and overlay, then choose a different next action. Prefer bridge state for scene/object facts and the screenshot for visual focus or UI diagnosis.
7. **Background Game Captures**: `GetWindowRect` plus `CopyFromScreen` captures the pixels currently visible on the desktop; it does not capture an occluded window. On Windows, use `PrintWindow(hwnd, memoryDC, 2)` and read the returned GDI bitmap to capture the game's window without calling `SetForegroundWindow`. Record the foreground window handle before and after and verify that it is unchanged. In this session it returned a 1920×1080 image while the game was behind Codex. A black fade can be the actual game UI state; pair the image with a read-only bridge scene/object query instead of retrying capture or input blindly.

### Controlled Steam Runtime Verification Protocol (AI & Card Rewards)
When validating mod content during live Steam sessions, follow these precise experimental protocols:
- **Reactive AI Counterattack Dispatch**:
  1. Confirm simulation time advances (`Time.timeScale == 1.0f`).
  2. Use a naturally obtained live Player combat unit with `canNotBeAttacked = false`, `canNotBeTargetedByAi = false`, and `doNotRegisterGlobally = false`; do not use the debug/test spawner.
  3. Issue a native Attack order targeting an AI building whose `aiBaseIndex` matches the active profile.
  4. Ensure `CurrentAttackTarget` references the target building while the attacker is within `defenseRadiusCells` (default 12 cells).
  5. Ensure AI base 0 has an uncommitted combat unit with attack and movement enabled, no current target, no paused skill activation, and an idle or Move command within `defenseRadiusCells`.
  6. Confirm the defender transitions to the attacker's position and initiates weapon firing. Do not claim reactive behavior from profile attachment alone.
- **Stage Blueprint Reward Sampling**:
  1. Progression route maps sample rewards from active stage pools via `ChooseCard.PresentCardsToChoose`.
  2. Modded factory blueprints appear only when active stage definitions declare their unit IDs.
  3. When an offer appears, verify that placing the factory deducts crystals, starts rollout upon completion, opens factory doors via `InProductionCount > 0`, and instantiates the unit with custom 3D visuals.

### Content-First Expansion Philosophy & Boundary Guarantees
- **Native World Progression Variants**: Modded stages currently operate as progression variants on native world templates (`27 Circles`, `MainSceneDesert`, etc.), injecting custom seeds, parameters, and reward pools additively.
- **Prefab & Script Containment**: The authoring project at `unity/RogueCommandAssetAuthoring/` cannot attach compiled game scripts from `Assembly-CSharp.dll` inside the Editor. Visual prefabs must remain script-free, relying entirely on `RuntimeVisualDirector` attached dynamically by the loader.
- **Release Packaging Invariant**: Rebuild the release DLL, validate manifests/schemas, build the 9-entry ZIP via `Package-Loader.ps1`, and verify hashes before distributing.

### Authoritative 8-File Reversible Runtime Deployment Table

The package has nine ZIP entries. Eight are runtime files under `BepInEx`; `INSTALL.txt` is a separate package document. During a controlled runtime test, extract only these eight runtime entries to the game root. The content directory is nested beneath the plugin's own directory; there are no `pack.json` files or generated loader config in this package.

| Target Index | Deployment Destination in Steam Installation | Source File / Origin | Post-Run Verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader.dll` | `artifacts/plugin/RogueCommandExpansionLoader.dll` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 2 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/packs/README.txt` | Generated by `tools/Package-Loader.ps1` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 3 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/packs/sample-native-expansion/content.json` | `content/packs/sample-native-expansion/content.json` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 4 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/packs/sample-native-expansion/bundles/assetbundle-manifest.bundle` | Native sample bundle manifest | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 5 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/packs/sample-native-expansion/bundles/sample-native-expansion.bundle` | `content/packs/sample-native-expansion/bundles/sample-native-expansion.bundle` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 6 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/packs/sample-reactive-ai/content.json` | `content/packs/sample-reactive-ai/content.json` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 7 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/schema/content.schema.json` | `content/schema/content.schema.json` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |
| 8 | `BepInEx/plugins/RogueCommandExpansionLoader/content/schema/README.md` | `content/schema/README.md` | Restore baseline SHA-256 |

## 8. Development & Packaging Workflow

### Local Build and Validation Commands
```powershell
# 1. Compile C# Loader and run complete validation suite (132 checks)
dotnet run --project tests/RogueCommandExpansionLoader.ValidationTests -- .

# 2. Rebuild Release Plugin DLL and create distributable 9-entry ZIP
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools/Package-Loader.ps1

# 3. Verify twin script binary parity (0 byte difference required)
fc.exe /b tools\RogueModelKit.py .agents\skills\rogue-command-3d-model-authoring\scripts\RogueModelKit.py
fc.exe /b tools\generate_unit_model.py .agents\skills\rogue-command-3d-model-authoring\scripts\generate_unit_model.py

# 4. Blender 4.5.14 dry-run archetype preflight & procedural features
# Supports --suspension-arms, --mantlet-boot, --spaced-skirts, and --wreckage-severity <light|medium|catastrophic>
.tools\Blender-4.5.14\blender-4.5.14-windows-x64\blender.exe --background --python tools/generate_unit_model.py -- --batch-all-archetypes --dry-run
```

### Unity 2021.3.45f2 Authoring Project Architecture
- **Location**: `unity/RogueCommandAssetAuthoring/`
- **Engine Baseline**: Unity `2021.3.45f2` (Mono Standalone x64, URP).
- **Preserved Metadata**: `ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset`, `ProjectSettings/TagManager.asset`, `Packages/manifest.json`, and Plastic SCM (`.plastic/`, `ProjectSettings/PlasticSCM.asset`). Never delete or overwrite with external project settings.
- **Headless Prefab Scaffolding Command**:
  ```powershell
  & "C:\Program Files\Unity\Hub\Editor\2021.3.45f2\Editor\Unity.exe" `
    -batchmode -quit `
    -projectPath "unity\RogueCommandAssetAuthoring" `
    -executeMethod RogueCommandExpansion.Authoring.VehiclePrefabBuilder.BuildPrefabFromCommandLine `
    -gameplayJson "artifacts\ColossusFortress.gameplay.json"
  ```
- **AssetBundle Build Command**:
  ```powershell
  & "C:\Program Files\Unity\Hub\Editor\2021.3.45f2\Editor\Unity.exe" `
    -batchmode -quit `
    -projectPath "unity\RogueCommandAssetAuthoring" `
    -executeMethod RogueCommandExpansion.Authoring.TacticalOperationsPackScaffolder.BuildTacticalOperationsPack
  ```
  Use RogueCommandVisualSampleScaffolder.BuildSampleNativeExpansion for the sample-native bundle (there is no AssetBundleBuilder class). Unity reserializes tracked .controller/.prefab files with line-ending churn during these builds; revert that noise before committing.

### Evidence Boundaries & Verification Discipline
- **Static Inspection**: Repository files, Python AST, managed assemblies via REA, Unity serialized asset inspection via UnityPy. Does not establish runtime execution.
- **Local Validation**: C# build output, validation test suite execution, ZIP archive audits, Blender background dry runs. Proves code correctness and packaging rules.
- **In-Game Runtime Evidence**: Observations from a live game session launched through Steam (AppID `1461910`). Only live evidence proves match functionality, card offers, factory production, and visual rendering.

### Git & Branch Policy
- Work committed on `codex/rogue-unit-balance-20261018` tracking `origin/codex/rogue-unit-balance-20261018`.
- Stage only task-owned paths explicitly (`git add <files>`); never use bulk `git add -A`.
- Keep BepInEx, Harmony, and game assemblies out of Git and distributable ZIPs.
